1       Summary of Performance
1.A Introduction
This Kano State Budget Implementation Report is prepared on a quarterly basis and issued within 30 days of
the end of each quarter. The report presents the original approved and revised (final) 2025 budget
appropriations for each organisational unit across the core economic classifications of expenditure—
Personnel Costs, Other Recurrent Costs, and Capital Expenditure. It also reports actual expenditures for the
fourth quarter (Q4), cumulative year-to-date expenditure, and the resulting balances against approved
revenue and expenditure appropriations.

The Q4 performance is assessed against the 2025 Revised Budget. Following a budget revision, the total
budget size increased by N215,378,374,543.62 billion, from the originally approved N719,755,417,663.00
billion to N935,133,792,206.62 billion. This revision resulted in an additional N50,595,288,123.11 billion for
recurrent expenditure and a N164,783,086,420.51 billion increase in capital expenditure, as reflected in the
third-quarter budget adjustment.

This Budget Implementation Report for Kano State is prepared quarterly and issued within 30 days from the
end of each quarter.

The core economic classifications, as per the National Chart of Accounts (NCOA) refer to:

    •   Revenue – Economic Account Type 1
    •   Personnel Cost Expenditure – Economic Sub-Account Type 21
    •   Overhead Cost Expenditure - Economic Account Class 2202
    •   Capital Expenditure - Economic Sub-Account Type 23
    •   Other Expenditures (covering Loans, Grants and Contributions, Subsidies, Debt Service and Transfer
        Payments) - Economic Account Classes 2203-2209 as applicable

This report includes sections 3 and 4 which presents a detailed reports on Primary Healthcare and Basic
Education expenditures, respectively. There is a Section that identifies the performance of climate change-
responsive capital expenditure related to adaptation and mitigation.
This Budget Implementation Report is produced by the Kano State Ministry of Planning and Budget in
collaboration with the Ministry of Finance, Kano, as well as consultant support from the Nigerian Governors
Forum and published on the Kano State website.

1.B Revenue Performance
The Recurrent Revenue, including Federation Account Allocation Committee (FAAC) and the internally
Generated Revenue (IGR). In the fourth Quarter (October – December) 2025, Kano State received
N86,266,485,115.44 as State FAAC Share, which includes Share of VAT, Statutory Allocation, Exchange
Gain, Ecological Fund, LNG Dividend, Electronic Money Transfer Levy (EMTL), Signature Bonus, and other
FAAC. While the cumulative performance of the FAAC from January to December 2025 is recorded
N384,555,848,370.63 as against the Revised Budget of N620,293,121,825.62, representing 62%. On the
side of Internally Generated Revenue, the performance is N30,578,363,867.91 for the quarter under
review, and the cumulative collection for the fourth quarter of the year is N102,258,691,496.49, indicating
96.9% achievement. The total amount received by the state on Total Recurrent Revenue for the fourth
quarter 2025 is N116,844,848,983.35, while the Cumulative performance from January to December 2025
is                 N486,814,539,867.12, recording 67.1 % achievement.

The capital receipt component, which including loan, Aids, and Grants, in the fourth Quarter State, received
N19,939,521,558.81, while the Cumulative performance from January to December 2025 is
N37,175,567,311.13, recording 37.4 % achievement.



Budget Implementation Report - 2025 Quarter 4                                                         Page | 3
 Kano State Government




1.C Recurrent Expenditure Performance
While Recurrent Expenditure comprises of Personnel Cost, Overhead Cost and other Recurrent expenditure
which include settlement of liabilities both Domestic and Foreign liabilities, Grant and Subsidies, etc.
The Performance of Recurrent Expenditure for the fourth quarter 2025 is N63,111,963,451.53 while
cumulative for the fourth quarter of the Year 2025 is N240,866,121,305.46 indicating 76.9% achievement.
While the Performance of Personnel Cost for the quarter under review is N31,496,498,470.99 and
Cumulative Performance is represented as N116,507,282,440.47 representing 75.8% for the period
January to December 2025.
On the other hand, the Overhead cost performance is N27,814,969,221.75 for the fourth quarter, while the
sum of N90,866,802,289.27 was paid as Overhead for the period of January to December 2025 representing
79.8 against the revised budget of N113,800,924,326.49. While other Recurrent Expenditure that includes
payment of public debt, both Domestic and Foreign, is depicted as N3,800,495,758.79 for the fourth quarter,
the cumulative is recorded as N33,492,036,575.72, representing 73.2% against the Revised/ final Budget of
N45,761,450,067.80.


1.D Capital Expenditure Performance
The Capital Expenditure is financed through transfer from the Recurrent Budget Surplus and Capital receipt
which include Grants, Aids, and Loans from both domestic and foreign to capital expenditure.
The performance of Capital Expenditure for the fourth quarter 2025 is N141,290,446,143.80, while
cumulative expenditure for the period of January to December 2025 is N343,125,976,135.67, representing
55.2% achievement compared with the revised Budget. Some MDAs are overperforming due to the variation
of estimates and implementation, other oversight in the process, and revalidation of the contract in capital
projects.

1.E Conclusions
Conclusively, the aggregate Budget performance for the fourth quarter 2025 (January – December) in the
area of Total Revenue is N633,990,107,178.25 against the revised budget of N935,133,792,206.62,
which indicating 67.8%, while the cumulative fourth quarter expenditure performance is
N583,992,097,441.13 (January – December) 2025, recording 62.5% achievement.

3       Primary Healthcare Budget Performance
3.A Overview
This is specifically an overview the Primary Health Care Implementation Performance Report
extracted from the general report for easy tracking, analysis and other necessary needs. It includes
the original and revised 2025 approved budget against the actual expenditures (Personnel,
Overheads, Capital, and Others for the quarter Q3, attributed to each line items in the primary health
care activities, as well as the cumulative expenditures for year to date, and balances against each
of the revenue and expenditure appropriations.

It is important to note that the budgetary vote for Primary Healthcare may not fully reflect the
total expenditure by the Kano State Government on this critical service delivery sub-sector,
due to poor utilisation of the function segment of the NCOA. Therefore, in the event of a budget
supplementation or revision, it is anticipated that adjustments will be made to the primary
healthcare budget lines to ensure that they more accurately reflect the government's actual
policy prioritisation and financial commitment to the sector in the 2025 fiscal year.

As depicted in the chart below, for the Fourth Quarter performance, personnel cost recorded 9.7 %
outturn, overhead cost 0.3%, while capital expenditure recorded 85% performance.

A summary of the performance Year to Date (Jan. – December, 2025) against the Revised budget
is provided below for the main economic classifications of expenditure, and 2025 against the
programme segment (Objective level).

4       Basic Education Budget Performance
4.A Overview
This is specifically an overview the Basic Education Implementation Performance Report extracted
from the general report for easy tracking, analysis and other necessary needs. It includes the original
and revised 2025 approved budget against the actual expenditures (Personnel, Overheads, Capital,
and Others) for the quarter Q4, attributed to each line items in the Basic Education activities, as well
as the cumulative expenditures for year to date, and balances against each of the revenue and
expenditure appropriations.

For the twelve-month period, January to December 2025, Basic Education personnel cost recorded
60.5% performance, overhead cost – 196.5% outturn, while capital expenditure recorded 81.8%
performance against the Revised budget.

It is important to note that the budgetary vote for Basic Education may not fully reflect the
total expenditure by the Kano State Government on this critical service delivery sub-sector.
This is might have been caused by poor utilization of the programme segment of the NCOA in the
2025 budget preparation. Therefore, in the event of a budget supplementation or revision, it
is anticipated that adjustments will be made to ensure the government's actual policy
prioritization of Basic Education is reflected in the 2025 fiscal year.
